# Performance Optimizations

Performance work on the DLRMv4 HSTU ranker: where each platform stands today, what
has been optimized to get there, and the measurement traps worth knowing about.
Companion to `examples/mlperf/dlrm_v4/README.md` (environment +
reproduction).

- **[Latest evaluations](#latest-evaluations)** — current measured status, one row per hardware × node count.
- **[Optimization log](#optimization-log)** — each incremental optimization, with its own measured delta and explicit commit status.
- **[Misc](#misc)** — rejected alternatives and measurement gotchas that can invalidate a benchmark.

Throughput numbers are global samples/sec across all ranks (`global_sps`).
The current MI350X headline is the convergence pair: **time-to-target**
(RUN_START→RUN_STOP at equal samples) and **whole-run training throughput**
(perf-elapsed after the 20-step startup window, eval excluded). The historical
**200-update calculation from `START_TS=0`** (median logged throughput at
endpoints 20–200, the rate ladder) measures only the start of a run, where the
gain is largest; it is used for per-optimization attribution and ranking, not
as the long-run gain. Paired feature effects and equal-work aggregate MLLOG
timing are reported separately. Each row records the run scope, hardware,
source, config, and software stack.

---

## Latest evaluations

**Current status per hardware × node count** — one latest supported measurement
per row. Use each row's matched comparisons for feature effects; different hosts, batch
sizes and training intervals prevent a controlled scaling comparison between
these snapshots. Superseded rows are replaced rather than stacked up; the change
history lives in the [optimization log](#optimization-log) below. The B200 row is
yambda-5b bf16; the MI350X rows run the same workload with every shipped MI350X
optimization at its gin default, the FP8 HSTU forward and backward among them
(gfx950-only; a non-gfx950 launcher turns the FP8 arms off by env). Each MI350X
row compares the latest build against the previous trunk on one allocation, both
arms starting from a flushed page cache, and its build cell names the items that
build adds. What each item does, its numerics class, its opt-out and its own
measured delta are in the optimization log. The row date is the measurement date.

**The platforms are not running the same code.** The MI350X stack runs both
halves of HSTU attention on hand-written FlyDSL kernels (`gfx950`-only; B200 is
Triton throughout), spells the embedding dedup expand with `gather`, pins
six BLAS keys for the measured FP8/bf16 projection paths — solution indices are
specific to the exact library build — quantizes the HSTU linear layers and attention to
`e4m3` behind `$DLRM_HSTU_FP8_FWD`, runs the attention-backward qk GEMM on k128
fp8 MFMA, persists the forward's e4m3 `uvqk` pack on all three layers, and
quantizes the **output-projection dgrad** to `e5m2` behind `$DLRM_HSTU_FP8_BWD`,
runs all five attention-backward `bwd_dkdv` GEMMs in e4m3 behind
`$DLRM_HSTU_FP8_BWD_FULL`, and removes the per-step blocking host reads that
drained the GPU queue behind `$DLRM_HSTU_FP8_SYNCFREE`.
Compare them as platform snapshots, not as a kernel-for-kernel comparison.

> **One row per hardware × node count, latest only.** Superseded rows are
> deleted rather than kept for history — the per-arm measurements live in the
> [Optimization log](#optimization-log), which is the record. The 2026-09-13
> single-node MI350X row (27,769 `global_sps`) was removed on 2026-09-17 under
> this rule, superseded by 2026-09-16; both of its arms keep their own
> optimization-log rows, so nothing measured was lost.

**ship16 submission compliance.** ship16 is the first build under the user's working data rule (MLPerf `training_rules.adoc` L67-68 as written: high-level statistics such as size distributions allowed, content not; read as banning per-sample and label-derived information). What ship16 adds or discloses:

- **(82) M4-OTF ships ON by user decision (2026-10-13 ~17:00 PDT); SWG pre-clearance is open.** Within-step dealing keeps each step's global anchor multiset and window order and changes only which rank computes which anchor, i.e. per-rank batch composition (training_rules L268 / L270; the SWG ask also covers L70, L258 and L67-68). N per anchor is computed after `RUN_START` by one builder per node from the step's own batch and passed as a memfd: no per-anchor table, no data file opened before the GO gate (online audit ONLINE_OK, code r4 `dbbd9d48`). N uses the label-bearing listen flag of prior events; its classification under L258 is part of the SWG ask. Cost-law constants come from synthetic uniform-length timing (job 71809, verdict of record NO_DECISION, disclosed); earlier revisions' real-trace constants are removed. Kill switch `DLRM_HSTU_FP8_M4=0`; the G4 review notes that the env switch alone hides the state from the gin, so an M4-OFF RCP or submission would flip the gin binding.
- **TSPEC table re-recorded on the ship16 tree** (`ops/triton/triton_spec_table.jsonl`, sha256 `9a828c9a`, 380 rows, header `source: synthetic`, generator sha256 `a506e6df` = the shipped `tspec_synth_data.py`; jobs 72718/72719/72720). It clears ship15.1's 5 `src_changed` `_dq8_sched_kernel` rows and covers the new kernels; every rank of every ship16 arm resolved its own table (0 unresolved).
- **Submission gates on the final tree (`8a4ff18e`).** G2 static scan PASS: nothing reads dataset content, per-sample tables, label-derived data or offline analysis before `RUN_START` (M4-OTF's in-run use of the listen flag is carried to G4). G4 rules review: 27 of 28 rows PASS; M4-OTF NEEDS SWG PRE-CLEARANCE (above). G3, the runtime pre-`RUN_START` file-open audit (M4 ON and OFF arms), is owed on the commit tree, and G1 (MLPerf `package_checker` / `result_summarizer` / `repo_checker`) runs on the ship16 RCP logs once that campaign exists; stager v2.1 needs a staging-only smoke across the RCP node pool first.

## Optimization log

One row per **incremental optimization**, with landed commits or explicit
uncommitted status and the measured baseline stated in that row. This is the change history;
[Latest evaluations](#latest-evaluations) above is the snapshot of where each
platform stands right now.

> ⚠️ **A row here must be self-contained. Never cite the evaluation row for a
> measurement.** That table keeps only the LATEST row per hardware × node
> count, so it is overwritten whenever something new ships — a row that said
> "see the evaluation row" for its convergence data silently loses that data
> the next time anyone ships. Inline the jobs and the numbers. Pointing at it
> for *structure* (as the sentence above does) is fine; pointing at it for a
> *fact* is not.

Perf numbers are each optimization's **own A/B**, both arms run on the same host
set and allocation, with the stated baseline enabled. They are still not
comparable across rows — different dates, hosts, and clock states — so do not
chain them into a cumulative speedup.

**MFU and HFU are quoted for completeness, not as independent evidence.** Both
are trainer proxies, `FLOPs/sample × local_sps / peak`; the trainer excludes
eval/checkpoint time from that rate. Neither distinguishes faster kernels from
less GPU idle time. With a fixed FLOP numerator, the proxy and throughput
deltas agree only when computed over the same buckets and aggregation. The
current MI350X rows quote the proxy (last trainer perf line, step 500) only from the short 200-step
A/B; their convergence arms run the submission logging profile, which logs no perf lines. Earlier rows quote one average of the rounded proxy logs across
ranks over early steps, while the headline is convergence time-to-target and
whole-run training throughput. The early-run ladder's stack-vs-trunk figure uses logged medians at
endpoints 20–200, corroborated by the ROBUST MLLOG rate; its per-item rung
increments use the ROBUST MLLOG rate. Those deltas need not match. A change to
executed work can also change HFU's numerator: the historical
**targets-only** row reports throughput up20.04% while HFU falls14.2%. These
proxies are not hardware-counter utilization.

| date | main optimizations | main commits | e2e perf | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-25 | **PADSPREAD: (118) padding triples spread over distinct table rows (ship17.1 item 121; bitwise).** Purpose: ship17 perf at small local batch. Padding triples u >= n_u name row u % U, not the last token. Removes a 2·(M_pad-n_u)-entry hot segment in the EC cast-gather backward (serial `_reduce_lpt` pieces, ~1 ms per reduce). Padding grads are exact zeros; outputs and grads bitwise. Opt-out: gin `DLRM_HSTU_FP8_PREPROC_DEDUP_PADSPREAD` (env `=0`). | `095cdae` | 1n ladder (97166, `c16-18`): **+1.4%** `global_sps` (69,452 -> 70,394).<br>ABBA vs off, GBS 8192: **+1.1 / +1.6 / +2.0%** 1n/2n/4n (96860 / 96859 / 96858).<br>Trace: `_reduce_lpt` -1.7 to -1.9 ms per rank-step. | Microbench (96835): item reduce 1.05 -> 0.27 ms at 32-GPU size. |
| 2026-10-25 | **HILO2 + LEAN-118 + LEAN-119: host-launch cuts in (118)/(119) (ship17.1 items 122-124; bitwise).** Purpose: ship17 perf at small local batch (host-bound step). HILO2: hi/lo grad split as 2 launches into one buffer. LEAN-118: int64 change-flag cumsum, cached arange prefix, hi/lo row copies folded, one-op overflow note. LEAN-119: cached code constants, UIH lengths reused, dead segsum zero-fill skipped. -34 launches per step (trace). Opt-outs: gin `DLRM_HSTU_FP8_PREPROC_DEDUP_HILO2`, `DLRM_HSTU_FP8_PREPROC_DEDUP_LEAN`, `DLRM_HSTU_FP8_ACTION_DEDUP_LEAN` (env `=0`). | `095cdae` | 4n host-bound nodes (96972, ABBA x3 vs PADSPREAD only): **+1.8%** `global_sps` (195,817 -> 199,355).<br>Traced pair, same allocation (97521, PADSPREAD only -> on): **GPU step -0.59 ms (-1.1%), critical rank -0.97 ms (-1.7%)**; idle -0.49 ms; launches 686 -> 652.<br>1n ladder (97166): +0.1 / +0.1 / -0.2% (noise; 1n not host-bound). | Gain only where the step is host-bound (small local batch, fast-GPU nodes). |
| 2026-10-16 | **PREPROC_DEDUP: c/a preprocessor on unique triples (ship17 item 118, Arbor actckpt; precision up + reduction order).** c/a MLPs run once per unique (item, artist, album) triple on M_pad rows (R = 2·M_pad in training: bf16 hi/lo copies), then gather to tokens; duplicate-token grads fp32 segment-summed. **Numerics:** c/a FP8 to BF16 + fp32 reduction order. User-approved compute reduction (2026-10-15). Supersedes the per-token c/a stack (E-M3a c/a, E-X2b, IX, IX-FP8F, X2H, X2L-B, E-M1 tail) while armed; fallback = ship16 path. Opt-out: gin `DLRM_HSTU_FP8_PREPROC_DEDUP` (env `=0`). | `9dd4b02` | L20 1n (e06-08): A/B **+9.13%** global_sps (steady +9.15%); GPU span **-9.472 ms (-7.68%)**; PDEDUP family -10.49 ms.<br>Cumulative PDEDUP family -15.66 / -15.56 / -15.97 ms per rank-step 1n/2n/4n; added `_reduce_lpt` +3.72 / +3.85 / +3.56 (paired medians) ms.<br>Counters, every rank: train==dedup, eval==eval_dedup, declined=disabled=overflow=0 (conv train/eval calls 1n 8120/1255, 2n 5180/875, 4n 3360/496). | Single A/B + one traced pair. The no-overflow bound n_u ≤ M_pad relies on the schema (one triple per item). If n_u > M_pad: wrong c/a for 1-2 steps, then OVERFLOW abort. MLPerf compliance review of dedup pending. |
| 2026-10-16 | **ACTION_DEDUP: action MLP on code rows, not tokens (ship17 item 119, Arbor rccl; reduction-order).** Action MLP runs on the 9 action codes (2^T+1, T=3; x2 hi/lo = 18 rows), recomputed every forward from current weights; each token gathers its code row onto the residual. Backward: fp32 per-code grad sum (one-hot bmm, fixed chunks). Forward: exact MLP, ≤ 2 bf16 ulp in the action term only. User-approved compute reduction. Opt-out: gin `DLRM_HSTU_FP8_ACTION_DEDUP` (env `=0`). | `9dd4b02` | s17_ladder_final L19 (1n, c16-18, (118) off): A/B **+1.34%** global_sps (steady +1.54%); traced GPU span **-1.238 ms** (-0.98%).<br>ladder3 L19 (77792): A/B **+1.42%**.<br>Glue cost (gather-add + segsum + plan): 2.99 / 3.41 / 3.34 ms per rank-step, 1n/2n/4n.<br>Cumulative ADEDUP family: **-5.66 / -5.97 / -6.01 ms** per rank-step, 1n/2n/4n. | Single A/B pair per rung. L19 ran without (118); net of displaced X2L-F pass (+0.84 ms). ADEDUP family overlaps PDEDUP's (E-M1 LN512 fwd, LN512 bwd and SwishLN256 bwd counted in both; only the per-token action encoder, 0.15-0.17 ms, is ADEDUP's alone). GPU per-token vs dedup grad compare (K2) deferred to ship18. |
| 2026-10-16 | **BSR: persistent MXFP6 fwd K=512 GEMMs with register-resident weights, ON at every node count (ship17 item 110, Arbor r14; bitwise).** VQK (N=1536), VK (N=1024), U (N=512) move from RING6 to one persistent kernel per site. Prebuild self-checks each site bitwise vs RING6; a failing site keeps RING6. Gin `"1n"` -> `"1"`. Opt-out: gin `DLRM_HSTU_FP8_FWD_MXFP6_BSR` (env `=0`). | `9dd4b02` | 1n rung L11 (e06-08): A/B **+1.37%** (steady +1.39%); GPU span **-1.859 ms (-1.42%)**; family -2.22 ms; per-call VQK 0.740 / VK 0.716 / U 0.659, 100% of calls faster; 6/6 pre-registered legs HIT.<br>Flip rungs: 2n 76937 span **-1.964 ms (-1.47%)**, ABBA +1.26%; 4n 77534 span **-1.822 ms (-1.33%)**, ABBA +1.29%.<br>Cumulative family (RING6 -> BSR): **-2.21 / -2.38 / -2.44 ms** per rank-step 1n/2n/4n. | 1n: single A/B pair + one traced pair. 2n/4n: one ABBA pair each. Family: one traced pair per scale. |
| 2026-10-16 | **SPF fold: pow2 score coefficient folded into the QK MFMA E8M0 scale (ship17 items 105/106, Arbor x4c19/r15; precision).** Calibration snaps each qkv scale up so the score coefficient is exactly 2^j (s_snap/s_cal < sqrt(2)). fwd_kernel_0 and bwd_dkdv (token tsc) drop the score multiply; the QK MFMA takes E8M0 A-scale byte 127+j. Precision change = the snap; the fold is bitwise vs the snapped control. (106) needs (105). An invalid (105) value fails at bootstrap; an invalid (106) value raises at the first FP8 backward. | 2026-10-14 | **M4-OTF: speed-aware within-step anchor dealing, N computed online (ship16 item 82, Arbor comm + x4c19; replaces ship15's withdrawn item 82; ON by user decision, SWG pre-clearance open).** Each step waits for its slowest GPU, and at the 1000 W cap per-GPU speed differs within an allocation. M4-OTF keeps each step's global anchor multiset and window order and changes only which rank computes which anchor, giving slower GPUs cheaper anchors; anchors move only between ranks with the same max N, so the normalizer is unchanged. Cost per anchor = b*N + c*N(N+1)/2 scaled by an in-run per-rank speed estimate (b = 38.157e-6 ms/token, c = 13.509e-9 ms/pair, fitted on synthetic uniform-length designs D3-D5 of job 71809; D9 holdout 90.59 vs 90.61 ms; verdict of record NO_DECISION, disclosed; the real-trace constants of earlier revisions are removed). N is computed after `RUN_START` by one builder per node from the step's own batch and passed as a memfd: no per-anchor table and no data file opened before the GO gate (online audit ONLINE_OK, code r4 `dbbd9d48`). It deals only after its speed estimator warms up (first dealt window 33-44). **Numerics:** not bitwise vs round-robin (per-rank fp32 summation order, dropout pairing, per-rank amax histories; same global batches). | 2026-10-09 | **Grouped preprocessor LayerNorm(512) tail, forward (ship11 item 30, Arbor actckpt `preln_e1`; the name is historical, and this is not activation checkpointing).** F1+F2 (`fuse_linear_norm`, `fuse_ln_residual`) are on, so each of the three preprocessor MLPs ends in a Linear + LayerNorm(512) pair, and F2's round-before-add residual chains the three pairs. The item moves the three last-pair Linears to the end of the MLP section. One grouped Triton launch (`_grouped_ln_residual_fwd_kernel`) then computes all three LayerNorms and the residual chain, reading each input once and writing the output once. Before this, three `_weighted_layer_norm_fwd` launches wrote the content output and the running sum to HBM, and the next launch read them back. The addmm calls are the same and nothing is recomputed. The backward runs the shipped per-pair body for the three pairs in autograd order on the same saved tensors, so its LayerNorm backwards still go through the (29) / (28)(b) / (14) dispatch. On non-ROCm builds the item declines and the shipped path runs. Cost: the three last-pair inputs stay live until the grouped node's backward finishes, which holds one extra [N, 512] bf16 at the end of the backward; the preprocessor forward peak drops by one. Arming: `armed OK: [preproc-grouped-tail] inputs=3 N=... ln512_pins=8x1/4x1 bitwise=yes rank=` appears on every rank (8/16/32 ranks at 1n/2n/4n, 0 `bitwise=no`). | 2026-10-09 | **FlyDSL weighted LayerNorm backward (ship11 item 29, GitHub PR #7).** Four weighted-LayerNorm backward variants now run as one FlyDSL main kernel plus a FlyDSL finalize (`ln_bwd_flydsl_d{D}…` / `ln_bwd_flydsl_fin_…`): the STU norms with the residual add, the targets-only STU norm of item (20)(e) without the residual, the preprocessor MLP output norms with the dx sum, and the MLP SwishLayerNorms with the dx sum. The dispatch tries FlyDSL before the Triton v1/v2/v3 choice. Rows interleave over a persistent grid. Partials stay in registers and reduce in LDS. Blocks take rows in an XCD-aware order, and the streamed loads and the dx store use a streaming cache policy. Both of those are bitwise-neutral and each has its own kill switch. Three cases stay on Triton: the layer-0 input-LayerNorm backward with the item (20)(d) mask/scale epilogue, the fp32 item-MLP LayerNorm, and any non-`gfx950` device. One switch, `DLRM_HSTU_FP8_BWD_ATTN_V2`, makes the env bootstrap append this item's tokens to item (19)'s gin-bound VALU / `MSPLIT_SCHED` / `DQ_SPLIT_OPT` lists and bind the dq-split workspace store layout and aux bits. What it changes: (a) the workspace store layout is `l128t` with aux `2`: the dq-split ds workspace is stored as full rows with no lane exchange (reads are re-addressed so each lane already holds its payload) and a non-temporal store policy; the bytes and addresses are the same. (b) `kb`: the K row stride becomes a compile-time constant, so the dq kernel loads K with buffer loads. (c) `xt,rs2,lc,ek,nk,wh`: a wider silu exchange, loop-invariant per-lane offsets, a narrower loop counter, a carried K-block scale select, no prologue K staging for the operand dq-split never reads, and an early workspace header load. (d) `nobr`+`mc`: the dk/dv epilogue stores unconditionally through descriptors that end at the sequence, and the overflow-mode clear moves to the fenced top of the drain. (e) `dzr`: workspace stores of padding rows past the sequence end are dropped; the dq kernel masks those rows on read and never stores them. (f) `bst`: score-MFMA program order is interleaved by operand. (g) `ob0,obj`: the out-GEMM B operands are read earlier. Together these shorten `bwd_dkdv` and the dq-split dq kernel. No MFMA or VALU work is skipped; `dzr` drops only stores of values nothing reads. | 2026-10-09 | **Attention forward kernel configs: FP8 FlyDSL forward pipeline and a 16-row block for the delta-query forward (ship11 item 26, Arbor x4c19).** Two independent switches. Each kernel module reads its switch on every call, and only the value "1" arms it. (a) `DLRM_HSTU_FP8_FWD_PIPE`: the e4m3 FlyDSL attention forward (`fwd_kernel_0`) compiles with a 128-row m-block over 4 waves instead of the previous 64-row block over 2 waves. It also issues the next n-iteration's K/V global loads one iteration early, and builds the P@V A-fragment from the silu values in registers instead of going through LDS. It is active only when the FP8 FlyDSL forward runs (`DLRM_HSTU_FP8_FWD` with `HSTU_ATTN_FWD=flydsl`). (b) `DLRM_HSTU_FP8_DELTA_FWD_BM16`: the delta-query attention forward of the targets-only final layer (`triton_cached_hstu_mha`, kernel still `_hstu_attn_fwd`) launches BLOCK_M=16 / BLOCK_N=32 / 2 warps / 2 stages instead of the pinned BLOCK_M=128 config. With one delta query per sequence, 127 of the 128 rows in the old tile were masked padding. (b) applies when DeltaSize <= 16 on ROCm, with TMA off and not under `TRITON_FULL_AUTOTUNE=1`; otherwise the pinned config runs. Arming (once per rank): `[hstu-fwd] DLRM_HSTU_FP8_FWD_PIPE=1 armed: BM128 waves4 kv_prefetch p_direct` and `[hstu_delta_fwd] DLRM_HSTU_FP8_DELTA_FWD_BM16=1 armed: delta-query forward uses BLOCK_M=16`. Both lines are in the ship11 marker set (39/39 present in the candidate logs at 1n/2n/4n; 16 of each in the 1n candidate log, 0 in the control log). **Numerics: bit-identical.** (a) runs the same MFMAs on the same operands; the taller block only adds zero-weight (p=0) products, so the output is bitwise identical for finite V. A non-finite V value could reach more causally earlier rows of its block. (b) each query row walks the same key blocks with the same MFMA shape; only the padding rows go away. **MLPerf disclosure:** BM16 does not compute the fully masked padding rows of a fixed tiling. | 2026-10-09 | **Bitwise elementwise/sort/gather/copy bundle (ship11 item 24, Arbor rccl_elem_m1).** Five independent switches. Each one replaces serial main-stream launches with fewer launches. (a) `DLRM_HSTU_FP8_EC_SORT32`: the EC backward segment argsort sorts the row ids as stable int32 keys instead of int64 keys. (c) `DLRM_HSTU_FP8_EC_GATHER_V2`: the EC expand forward gathers (dedup reverse indices, including the item (13) cat-fused member) run as one multi-row Triton row copy with non-temporal stores. Before, they ran as several aten gathers. Small index tensors stay on `torch.gather`. (d) `DLRM_HSTU_FP8_WLN_BWD_POW2`: item (14)'s v2 weighted-LN backward forms its row means with an exact power-of-two multiply instead of a runtime-int divide when D is a power of two. It reaches only the v2 calls that (28)/(29) leave on v2. (e) `DLRM_HSTU_FP8_WSHADOW_FUSED`: stale FP8 weight shadows are refreshed by two Triton launches (amax partials, then scale/transpose/e4m3 cast), batching every stale shadow on the same device and stream. Before, an aten pack/amax/scale/quantize chain ran for each weight. (f) `DLRM_HSTU_FP8_DDP_BATCHED_COPY`: dense DDP copies gradients into its buckets with torch's `batched_grad_copy` (one foreach copy plus one flat divide per bucket) instead of one scaled copy per parameter. Arming, once per rank: `DLRMV4 armed OK: ec sort32`, `DLRMV4 armed OK: ec gather v2`, `armed OK: wln_bwd_pow2=1`, `DLRMV4 armed OK: fp8 wshadow fused (batch=1)`, `DLRMV4 armed OK: ddp batched copy`. Every switch printed its line on 8/16/32 of 8/16/32 ranks in the 1n/2n/4n candidate convergence arms. **Numerics: bit-identical.** (a) gives the same stable permutation, because the int32 key preserves order for any row id the table can hold. (c) copies the same bytes. (d) a multiply by a power of two rounds the same as the divide. (e) uses the same amax, scale formula, clamp and round-to-nearest-even e4m3 bytes as the aten chain. | 2026-10-07 | **MLPerf cache flush made real (ship7 item 17).** Rules L237: "You must flush the cache or restart the system prior to benchmarking ... This explicitly excludes RAM." Until now no launcher, RCP script or Slurm hook flushed the page cache while the trainer logged `cache_clear: True` on every fresh run (the checker accepts any value), and the 389 GB staged dataset fits in host RAM, so every earlier MI350X time-to-target was measured with a warm cache. `scripts/launch_slurm.sh` now runs `sync && sudo -n sysctl vm.drop_caches=3` on every node right before the trainer (NVIDIA's `CLEAR_CACHES` template; prints Cached before/after; a failed flush aborts), `run_and_time.sh` does the same, and `train_ranker.py` logs `cache_clear` with the real outcome (`MLPERF_CACHE_CLEARED`) and warns when false. **Numerics: none.** | **`ab29397`** (ship7, on trunk `3c3ed6e`): `scripts/launch_slurm.sh`, `run_and_time.sh`, `train/train_ranker.py`, gin item (17) (comment only: a host-side step, not a gin knob). | **Cost of a real flush on the ship6 tree, cold vs warm, production shape cut at 20 min, ABBA (2 cold + 2 warm arms per node count):** 1n **+4.6 s** (job 60853, `c23-18`), 2n **+12.3 s** (job 60854, `c18-08,e10-18`), a fixed startup offset (the gap at 5M samples equals the gap at 25M/50M); steady rate unchanged. Startup excess = timed-region time before steady state (`tools/eval_startup_cost.py`); mean ms/step over steps 50–290 with eval intervals removed; eval pass = median `[boundary] total_eval`. 1n job 58110/`c15-08`, 2n job 58111/`c12-08`+`c16-18`, 4n job 58268/`c15-08`+`c08-08`+`e09-08`+`e09-18`: 10/10 scored arms each, arming markers checked per arm, ECC 0 at allocation start, all GPUs `auto` before every arm. At 4n the 33 windows hold only about 60 updates per arm, so steps 50–290 contain 2 steady intervals: 4n step increments are unresolved (indicative only), and 4n startup excess uses `eval_startup_cost.py --gbs 32768 --steady-from 20 --steady-to 140` (R0 113.5 / 114.5, R1 112.7 / 112.8, R2 121.5 / 118.0, R3 103.1 / 111.4, R4 48.6 / 54.8 s, forward / reverse); 4n eval pass R0 10.21, R1 10.48, R2 7.91, R3 8.00, R4 7.04 s. **Traces:** traced candidate arm of each final allocation (candidate defaults + `OUTPUT_TRACE=1`, `NUM_TRAIN_TS` 38 / 38 / 44 = 555 / 271 / 266 updates, warm container), stitched with `tools/recommendation/dlrm_v4/stitch_traces.py`, installed as `traces/mi350x/{1,2,4}_n/trace_step52.json.gz` (SHA256 in `final/layout/sha256.txt`), layout-checked against the previous committed six-item-stack traces (`final/layout/check_*.txt`); the 4n check ran on a locally patched copy because `trace_layout_check.py` raises a TypeError in `json.dumps(..., sort_keys=True)` on mixed int/None evidence keys (tool bug, not fixed in the tree). **Failed attempts (earlier pairs):** 2n job 58265 (`e16-18`+`e12-18`) stopped in the control warmup (rank SIGSEGV at init and an RCCL connect failure, `conv_off/2n_segv_58265_e12-18`); 4n jobs 58103 and 58252 aborted at arming checks because rank-0-only `gc_freeze` lines were lost from the shared console+MLPerf log (`conv/4n_armfail_58103_lostline`, `conv_off/4n_lostline_58252`). **Shared-log caveat:** with `LOG == MLPERF_LOG_PATH`, rank 0's MLPerf logger truncates the file at init while the launcher tee appends, so rank-0 lines and MLLOG records can be lost or torn (the two aborts above; the 2n job 58285 console logs lost `run_start` and some `train_loss` events, which its per-arm MLPerf logs keep); the final pairs give every arm its own MLPerf log, and submission runs must do the same. **Allocation variance (earlier logging-off pairs, context only; ship4 with training metrics off but `train_loss` every 5 and the scale log on; `conv_off/SUMMARY.md`):** 1n job 58245/`c23-18` 2691.0 → 2380.9 s (−11.52%); 2n job 58285/`e10-08`+`e10-18` 1842.8 → 1536.6 s (−16.61%, per-arm MLPerf logs); 4n job 58284/`c16-18`+`c18-08`+`e12-08`+`e21-08` 1427.3 → 1301.8 s (−8.80%; slow allocation, candidate startup 77.4 s). Those controls lie 1.2% / 0.4% / 8.3% above the final ones; the candidate varies more across allocations, as its eight-worker eval passes and early training are host-sensitive (2n eval passes ~21.5 s on average in 58285 against 28.2–31.0 s in 58634; 1n 58633 had two slow passes of 50.7 and 63.0 s against 40.7–41.3 s). The run shape (LR warmup, cold containers, production eval cadence) differs from the PR #907 and six-item pairs, so seconds do not compare across those rows. | 2026-10-03 | **Host-sync-free training step (G).** Removes the per-step blocking host reads that drained the GPU queue: GAUC `max_length` `.item()`, `fx_length_stats` `.tolist()`, the MLPerf train-loss `float()` and torchrec's KJT `length_per_key` device-to-host copy. Length statistics and `length_per_key` come from host lengths before the H2D copy (the per-key counts ride the splits all-to-all that already runs); the loss is read one step late from a pinned copy (same value, same MLLOG order); a one-step event fence bounds the host lead. One configuration at every node count: the forward's `dist_init` runs on the main stream (with `record_stream` on the all-to-all buffers), and the deferred splits wait is issued only after a poll of `Work.is_completed()`. Gin `DLRM_HSTU_FP8_SYNCFREE=True`; `0` restores trunk behavior. Requires the FlyDSL current-stream launch. **Numerics: same math** — the same integers and kernels on every sample; only when the host reads values changes. | **`cb34303`** (on trunk `e0849f8`): new `dlrm_v4/host_sync_free.py`; `datasets/dataset.py`, `dlrm_v4/utils.py`, `train/utils.py`, `train/mlperf_logging_utils.py`, `modules/dlrm_hstu.py`, `ops/flydsl/__init__.py`, `trace_postprocessing.py` (exports a host-ahead step by its CPU extent), `train/_env_bootstrap.py`, `gin/yambda_5b.gin`. The profiler's CPU-side tracing (~59 µs per `hipLaunchKernel`) removes the host lead G depends on, and once the host runs ahead, a CPU ProfilerStep no longer brackets its own GPU work. In the pre-stack validation, the 2n MLLOG rate of G vs off inside profiler windows was −9.9%..+5.7% against +6.9..+9.6% outside. At 2n G trails a stream-priority variant by −0.34% ROBUST, narrowly outside the 0.33% mean control spread (allocation g2a −0.36% vs 0.22%); 0.00% all-interval, within spread. The stack's ladder gain (+8.42% / +8.81% / +9.38%) exceeds its whole-run gain (+5.91% / +5.71% / +6.29%) because the ladder covers only the first windows, where the per-step saving is largest (1n convergence pair: 20.5 ms/step saved in the first tenth of the run, 12.6 ms in the last; its steps 40–540 reproduce the ladder's +8.6%), and because at 2n/4n the ladder ran on different hosts (over steps 40–260 its trunk arms were 1.3% / 4.1% slower than the convergence control, and the ladder reads 1.3 / 2.5 points above the convergence pair). **G does not miscount steps or samples:** `samples_count` is host-counted once per trained step (`num_candidates × world_size`), every perf window ends with blocking syncs in both arms, and RUN_STOP samples are equal; the only artifact is that MLLOG `train_loss` timestamps are emitted one cadence interval late (20 steps in convergence, 1 in the ladder), with correct values and samples, which does not affect time-to-target. Checkpoint save/resume was off in every measured arm. Does not reduce computation.<br><sub>**Shared method for the six 2026-10-03 rows.** One allocation per topology; cumulative rungs on the candidate tree via env overrides: R0 all six off, R1 +GC freeze, R2 +current stream, R3 +C8, R4 +C4, R5 +C5, R6 +sync-free (= gin defaults); T = clean trunk tree. Traced ladder 55475 repeats the 1n ladder with the profiler (ProfilerStep steps 53–56, 32 rank-steps; GPU-iteration 3 iterations, 24 pairs). Traced ladders 55999 (2n, 17/17 arms; 64 / 48 pairs) and 56105 (4n; 128 / 96 pairs) do the same; in 56105 arm TR2a crashed at RCCL init on one node (HIP `invalid device pointer`) before training, so the 4n forward pairs of the current-stream and C8 increments are missing. 4n control cross-rank spread (all per-item pairings) is 2.2–9.8% (≤0.21% at 2n), context only.</sub> |

| 2026-10-03 | **Full-FP8 attention backward with block dO scales (C8).** All five FlyDSL `bwd_dkdv` GEMMs run on the gfx950 f8f6f4 MFMA (k128). dO is quantized to e4m3 with one power-of-two scale per (sequence, head, 32-token group) and the scale is undone inside the MFMAs through their E8M0 scale operands; the e4m3 casts of silu and ds saturate at ±448 (the gfx950 converter returns NaN on overflow). Root cause on dumped real tensors: dO rows span about 1e4, 99.6% of rows sit more than 100× below the candidate rows, 70% of rows flushed entirely to zero under the per-tensor scale (87–96% of nonzero dO elements), so small-row dq fell to 1–6 dB SNR against 78–79 dB for bf16. Energy-weighted SNR (32–41 dB) and the synthetic microbench hid it. Block scales lift small-row dq to 23–30 dB; dk/dv stay at the e4m3 floor (28–31 dB), which costs no accuracy: eval 0.6353 / 0.6548 / 0.6849 against trunk 0.6352 / 0.6549 / 0.6846, deltas of mixed sign. Block-scale kernel cost +0.15%, quantizer −0.4 ms. | 2026-09-16 | • the dedup expand's backward stops being<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;`scatter_add_` and becomes a **sorted segment<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;reduce** (`EC_SEGMENT_REDUCE`, **on by default since 2026-09-16**)<br>• one Triton kernel: argsort the destination<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;rows, then one program per output row folding<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;its segment with `tl.sum` — **no atomics** on the<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;cold path<br>• `num_warps=2` and a chunked `[BLOCK_S, BLOCK_W]`<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;gather; each is worth ~5× on its own<br>• rows longer than `enable_segment_reduce.split_above`<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;(1024) split into pieces that `atomic_add`, for<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;load balance<br>• **LANDED, default ON since 2026-09-16** (landed default OFF at `3546d73`)<br>• **on by default**; opt OUT with `EC_SEGMENT_REDUCE=0`.<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;Requires `ec_bf16_output`, and **raises** without it | **Landed** at `3546d73`<br>`dlrm_v4/train/ec_segment_reduce.py` (**new**, the Triton kernel; its `configure_segment_reduce` is a **plain setter, deliberately not a gin configurable** — see the note under the table),<br>`dlrm_v4/train/ec_bf16_output.py` (`_segment_reduce_backward`, and the `enable_segment_reduce` gin surface that forwards the geometry),<br>`train/gin/yambda_5b.gin`, `dlrm_v4/train/utils.py`,<br>`scripts/launch_slurm.sh` (env allow-list forwards) | **−11.13 ms/step** (**−3.88%**)<br>286.65 → **275.52** `ms/step`<br>28,578 → **29,733** `global_sps`<br>paired median **+4.37%** (57 points, steps ≥ 20)<br>per round **+5.40 / +3.99 / +4.70%**, **3/3** rounds positive<br>MFU 49.00 → **51.00%**, HFU 14.80 → **15.80%** (paired **+4.37 / +4.17%**)<br>paired t = **−10.77** (57 points), faster at **54/57**<br>null floor **0.95%** — this row clears it **4.6×** | **The atomics were the cost, not the bandwidth.** The expand backward scatters ~7.9M token rows into ~54k unique rows, and `scatter_add_` serialises them through global atomics. Sorting the destinations first makes each output row a contiguous segment that one program folds with `tl.sum` — no atomic on the cold path.<br><br>**Two Triton levers, each ~5×, both gfx950-specific.** A 30-point sweep spread **10×** on this one kernel. `num_warps=2` won all 10 (BLOCK_S, BLOCK_W) cells by 1.9–4.8× — 8 warps put two waves on a SIMD at ~256 VGPR and spill to scratch. Replacing the per-row `tl.load` chain with a chunked `[BLOCK_S, BLOCK_W]` gather collapses ~100 dependent HBM latencies per segment to one per chunk (BLOCK_S 4 → 32: 3.70 → 1.49 ms).<br><br>**Better conditioned than what it replaces.** `scatter_add_` accumulates in nondeterministic order and has no bits to match, so both are scored against an fp64 `index_add_` reference: the segment kernel's max error is **0.61×** the incumbent's. All four rungs agree on `train_loss` to five significant figures at every logged step.<br><br>**Convergence: delta 0 on 3 seeds — this is the row that owed the gate**, the only one of the three that reorders an accumulation. Three paired seeds, ctl+cand co-located per seed (jobs **38072** `e12-18`, **38075** `c11-08`, **38076** `e18-08`): samples to AUC 0.75 **identical seed for seed, delta 0** — **68,812,800 / 75,694,080 / 61,931,520**, crossing AUC 0.7505–0.7513; convergence wall clock **1.146×** median (1.146 / 1.122 / 1.150×). `mlperf_logging` `003087f` ruleset 6.1.0: `compliance_checker` **SUCCESS 6/6**, `rcp_checker` passes both groups.<br><sub>The standalone **6.28×** (2.668 → 16.763 ms, sort included) was measured on a *uniform* duplicate draw. The production distribution is violently skewed — p50 **43**, p99 **1,439**, max **64,326** duplicates on a single row (job 38100) — so the standalone figure does not transfer; only the +4.37% e2e stands. `DLRMV4_EC_SEG_SPLIT`=1024 sits between p99 and p999 and exists for **load balance, not occupancy**: at ~54k output rows the grid already fills the device. Ladder job **37996** on `c13-08`: baseline → +F1+F2 → +ec_bf16_output → +segment_reduce, 3 counterbalanced rounds of 200 steps (odd rounds ascend, even descend); each row is the paired per-window median against the rung below, steps ≥ 20, n=57; null floor = same-rung pairs across rounds; 8/8 `auto` and every knob asserted present and absent; a cut-off fourth round is excluded.</sub> |
| 2026-09-16 | • the EC's jagged output is produced **in bf16**<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;rather than fp32-then-cast (`EC_BF16_OUTPUT=1`)<br>• the cast is hoisted to **before** the expand, so it<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;runs on 69k unique rows instead of 7.1M tokens<br>• the backward keeps an **fp32 accumulator**: the<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;hoist trades no accuracy<br>• key segments under `configure_ec_bf16_output.min_rows`<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;(100k) keep their original dtype<br>• **LANDED, default ON since 2026-09-16** (landed default OFF at `3546d73`)<br>• **on by default**; opt OUT with `EC_BF16_OUTPUT=0`.<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;Requires `ec_gather_select`, and **raises** without it | **Landed** at `3546d73`<br>`dlrm_v4/train/ec_bf16_output.py` (**new**),<br>`dlrm_v4/train/utils.py` (which imports it **eagerly** — that is load-bearing, see the note under the table), `modules/dlrm_hstu.py`,<br>`train/gin/yambda_5b.gin`, `scripts/launch_slurm.sh` | **−15.91 ms/step** (**−5.26%**)<br>302.56 → **286.65** `ms/step`<br>27,075 → **28,578** `global_sps`<br>paired median **+4.99%** (57 points, steps ≥ 20)<br>per round **+4.36 / +4.99 / +5.07%**, **3/3** rounds positive<br>MFU 46.50 → **49.00%**, HFU 14.10 → **14.80%** (paired **+5.06 / +4.96%**)<br>paired t = **−13.05** (57 points), faster at **55/57**<br>null floor **0.95%** — this row clears it **5.3×** | **The cast was on the wrong side of the expand.** The EC emits fp32, the transducer wants bf16, and the cast ran on the *expanded* tensor — 7.1M token rows — when the same cast on the *unique* rows is 69k, a **103×** smaller tensor for identical results. Pure deletion of HBM traffic: the largest of the three rows from the smallest change.<br><br>**No accuracy cost.** The backward casts forward to bf16 but zeroes an **fp32** accumulator and scatters into it, so the reduction — ~100 values land on each row — never leaves fp32.<br><br>**It refuses to be silently inert.** Only meaningful with `EC_INDEX_DEDUP=1`, which is what creates the expand at all; the patch raises rather than accepting an argument it cannot honour.<br><br>**Convergence: delta 0 on 3 seeds.** Three paired seeds, ctl+cand co-located per seed (jobs **38072** `e12-18`, **38075** `c11-08`, **38076** `e18-08`): samples to AUC 0.75 **identical seed for seed, delta 0** — **68,812,800 / 75,694,080 / 61,931,520**, crossing AUC 0.7505–0.7513; convergence wall clock **1.146×** median (1.146 / 1.122 / 1.150×). `mlperf_logging` `003087f` ruleset 6.1.0: `compliance_checker` **SUCCESS 6/6**, `rcp_checker` passes both groups.<br><sub>Ladder job **37996** on `c13-08`: baseline → +F1+F2 → +ec_bf16_output → +segment_reduce, 3 counterbalanced rounds of 200 steps (odd rounds ascend, even descend); each row is the paired per-window median against the rung below, steps ≥ 20, n=57; null floor = same-rung pairs across rounds; 8/8 `auto` and every knob asserted present and absent; a cut-off fourth round is excluded.</sub> |
| 2026-09-16 | • **F1** `fuse_linear_norm`: the layer-norm folds<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;into the preceding linear's epilogue (3 sites)<br>• **F2** `fuse_ln_residual`: the residual add folds<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;into that same epilogue<br>• **F2 is not separable from F1** — it fuses into the<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;epilogue F1 creates, so F2-alone is a no-op. One<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;rung, one row, both knobs<br>• **LANDED, default ON since 2026-09-16** (landed default OFF at `3546d73`)<br>• **on by default**; opt OUT with `FUSE_LINEAR_NORM=0 FUSE_LN_RESIDUAL=0` — **both**, or it raises<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;(or the two gin params) | **Landed** at `3546d73`<br>`ops/fused_linear_norm.py` (**new**, the fused kernel),<br>`ops/triton/triton_layer_norm.py`, `modules/preprocessors.py`,<br>`dlrm_v4/train/utils.py`, `train/gin/yambda_5b.gin`,<br>`scripts/launch_slurm.sh` | **−4.76 ms/step** (**−1.55%**)<br>307.32 → **302.56** `ms/step`<br>26,656 → **27,075** `global_sps`<br>paired median **+1.76%** (57 points, steps ≥ 20)<br>per round **+1.60 / +2.10 / +1.60%**, **3/3** rounds positive<br>MFU 45.80 → **46.50%**, HFU 13.80 → **14.10%** (paired **+1.72 / +2.01%**)<br>paired t = **−6.48** (57 points), faster at **49/57**<br>null floor **0.95%** — this row clears it only **1.9×** | **The weakest of the three, and the one to read most carefully.** +1.76% is real — 3/3 rounds positive, t = **−6.48**, per-round spread (1.60 / 2.10 / 1.60%) tighter than either row above — but it clears the **0.95%** null floor only **1.9×**, where the other two clear it 4.6× and 5.3×. A small effect established by replication, not a figure to quote to three digits.<br><br>**F2 has no standalone existence.** `fuse_ln_residual` folds the residual into the layer-norm epilogue `fuse_linear_norm` creates; with F1 off there is nothing to fold into. One rung, one row.<br><br>**Convergence: delta 0 on 3 seeds.** Three paired seeds, ctl+cand co-located per seed (jobs **38072** `e12-18`, **38075** `c11-08`, **38076** `e18-08`): samples to AUC 0.75 **identical seed for seed, delta 0** — **68,812,800 / 75,694,080 / 61,931,520**, crossing AUC 0.7505–0.7513; convergence wall clock **1.146×** median (1.146 / 1.122 / 1.150×). `mlperf_logging` `003087f` ruleset 6.1.0: `compliance_checker` **SUCCESS 6/6**, `rcp_checker` passes both groups.<br><sub>Triton's autotune cache key includes argument dtypes, which is why an earlier F2 attempt failed its bit-identity gate — the residual and plain variants collided on one cache entry. Engagement<br>verified on all 8 ranks of every test arm.</sub> | **Why this is worth 0.345% and not the 1.85% the site was projected to give.** The projection came from an offline probe that timed the dgrad against a **stock** bf16 GEMM. Production does not run a stock GEMM here: `out_dgrad` is one of the five `GR_PIN_BF16_BLAS_SOLUTIONS`, and the control's kernel is a pinned `Custom_Cijk_...` solution. Against the pinned baseline the FP8 GEMM is **1.45x**, not the 2.67x the probe measured, so the pin had already banked most of the win. The cast is likewise **1.172 ms** rather than the 0.656 ms assumed. Both facts are read off the paired traces, not modelled.<br><br>**Only the dgrad is quantized, and that is a layout result rather than a preference.** `hipblaslt_scaled_addmm` requires both operands contiguous along K. For the dgrad, K is the small feature dimension: the gradient is already row-major and the weight shadow is built once per optimizer step, so nothing has to be transposed. For the wgrad, K is the 2.4-million-row dimension and **both** operands would need re-laying out; the transposed quantization runs at ~140 GB/s against ~850 GB/s row-major, so the conversion costs more than the GEMM saves. `torch._scaled_mm` additionally rejects a `mat1` whose trailing dimension is not a multiple of 16, which for the wgrad is the jagged row count. The targets-only compact-UQ pair was dropped for a different reason: at M = 1,024 it costs **0.01 ms**, four significant figures below everything else, so there is no win to have.<br><br>**`e5m2` for the gradient, and this was learned the expensive way.** The campaign first chose `e4m3` for gradients as well as weights, on spike data - evidence that only describes the top of the range. In a float format the margin costs nothing in mantissa, so an 8x margin is paid entirely at the bottom: `e4m3` rounds **56.55%** of this gradient to zero, Adam normalizes per parameter and gives a flushed coordinate no update at all, and three paired seeds came in **2.22% of samples** behind the control. `e5m2` spends one mantissa bit for four decades of range and takes that 56.55% to **0.04%**. Weights stay `e4m3`: their range is narrow, so for them the mantissa bit is the better buy.<br><br>**The scale is refreshed, not frozen, and the amax is free.** The forward arm calibrates four steps then freezes, which is what removes the per-step amax and the blocking device-to-host read from its steady-state step. That policy does not transfer: the gradient amax **grows 521x** over training, so a frozen scale lands at 0.85-0.98 relative error. The controller refreshes on a ~200-step cadence at an 8x margin - the margin does the work, because single-step spikes reach 4.66x and no cadence tracks a spike. A separate reduction pass over a 10 GB tensor would cost about what the GEMM saves, so the amax is computed **inside the cast**, which already reads every element; it rides along for between -0.2% and +1.6% of the cast.<br><br>**Read the median, not the mean.** The two estimators are computed over identical logs and differ by 2x. Step-to-step spread is ~33 ms on a ~340 ms mean - an order of magnitude larger than the effect - so the mean is partly counting how many slow window-boundary steps each arm hit, and it is the estimator that moves when the arm order is unbalanced. The median is robust to both. The trace-attributed 0.345% falls inside the median's interval and outside the mean's point estimate, which is the independent check that settles it.<br><br>**What is and is not established.** Convergence is now measured, not argued: three paired seeds reach AUC 0.75 at identical sample counts against an FP8-forward control. What remains unestablished is resolution -- a gain this size is close to the limit of a 200-step harness: an earlier 23-pair campaign on this same arm read **-0.185%** (95% CI [-1.19, +0.82]) and its 2.57% claim was withdrawn. The kernel-level accounting above is what makes the present figure credible, not the rate on its own.|

The obstacle to using that is the cache key: TunableOp keys on the full problem descriptor, which includes **M, the total jagged token count** — and M changes every step (2229337, 2376002, 2284350, 2365056 across four consecutive traced steps), so a tuning file collected at one step never hits at the next. Everything *else* in the key is M-independent and the winning solutions proved stable across token counts, so `ops/blas_solution_pins.py` synthesises the cache line for whatever count the batch has and merges it with `tunable.read_file`. **~0.3 ms of CPU per new token count**, which the GPU covers.<br><br>**Five of six ship** — every one that actually beats the default. Each pin below carries three numbers: what the offline sweep promised (speedup, and the `ms/step/rank` it implies against the 361 ms step), what the call site actually did **in situ** (trunk → pinned, from the paired step-52 traces), and how its output compares against the heuristic's — each pin installed **alone**, across **eight token counts from 1024 to 2.36 M** including deliberately awkward values, against a reference captured in a separate process before any pin is installed.<br>• **`uvqk_fwd`** — sweep 1.37x, 7.88 ms; in situ **29.29 → 27.55** (1.06x); **3.4e-3**, one to two bf16 ULPs<br>• **`uvqk_wgrad`** — sweep 1.44x, 4.67 ms; in situ **15.01 → 9.44** (1.59x); **bit-identical**<br>• **`out_dgrad`** — sweep 1.31x, 2.08 ms; in situ 9.30 → 8.64 (1.08x); **1.7e-3**<br>• **`out_wgrad`** — sweep 1.26x, 2.05 ms; in situ **10.63 → 6.75** (1.57x); **bit-identical**<br>• **`out_fwd`** — sweep 1.06x, 0.56 ms; in situ 11.97 → 11.74 (1.02x); **4.2e-3**<br>• `uvqk_dgrad` — **not shipped**; sweep 1.01x, 0.07 ms; in situ 9.14 → 8.55, the control; 1.8e-3<br><br>No pin produced a NaN or an Inf at any tested token count, and none changed a sign or an exponent — every difference is in the last mantissa bit or two. `uvqk_dgrad` is omitted because 5.29 against 5.33 ms is a wash and it is the shape whose earlier pin trained to NaN, so it carries risk for no gain; leaving it unpinned is also what makes it the control in the in-situ column, since it is the one call site that should not move. The MLP forward GEMM is absent for a stronger reason — tuning picked a solution **1.7x slower** than the default there.<br><br>**Prediction and measurement agree, which is the main thing this row has to offer.** Those five total **17.25 ms/step/rank = 4.78%** of the step if every millisecond lands on the critical path, or **4.06%** at the **0.85** pass-through rate the earlier kernel work measured. Measured e2e: **4.05%**. A kernel saving that arrives whole is the exception in this document — the FlyDSL forward row passes ~40–55% and the targets-only row *overshoots* because removing compute uncovers collectives — so the agreement is worth stating rather than assuming. It is what you would expect from a change that makes existing GEMMs faster without moving, removing or reordering any work: `fill` and `exec` are byte-identical between the arms, so the two runs executed the same FLOPs in the same shape. The trace confirms the mechanism directly — **GPU busy union falls 349.89 → 335.53** `ms/step/rank`, tracking the −14.79 ms step delta rather than a fraction of it, which is what says the saving sits on the critical path instead of under overlap.<br><br>**The trace shows the pin engaging by kernel identity, not by timing.** A pin does not make a GEMM faster, it selects a *different* hipBLASLt kernel, so the check that it worked is a name. In the pinned arm's step-52 trace `uvqk_fwd` runs `Cijk_Ailk_Bljk_..._MT256x224x64_...` where the trunk arm runs `MT256x256x64`, and `out_dgrad` runs `Custom_Cijk_Alik_Bljk_..._shortname0_gfx950` where the trunk runs a stock `Cijk_`. Neither appears anywhere in the other arm's trace. That is the liveness evidence; a pin that silently failed to install would show the trunk kernel and an unchanged step time, and those two failure modes are indistinguishable in a rate alone. <sub>The `Custom_` prefix is also a trap for trace tooling: an analyzer that finds GEMMs by matching `Cijk_` drops that kernel entirely and reports the call site as having vanished (261x). Attribute by the launching `cpu_op`, not by kernel name.</sub><br><br>**In situ the pins do not rank the way the offline sweep ranks them**, which is the caveat to carry forward and the reason both columns are listed above. Summed over the five, the traces give **−12.09 ms/step/rank**. The offline sweep had made `uvqk_fwd` the single largest item at 7.88 ms and 1.37x; in a real step it returns **1.75 ms at 1.06x**, while the two wgrad pins together return **9.45 ms**, more than the sweep credited them. Eight ranks contending for L2 and HBM is not one idle GPU — the same lesson the FlyDSL forward row records for `MT=2`. **`uvqk_dgrad`, which is *not* pinned, moves −0.59 ms (1.07x) across the same pair**, and that is the built-in control: it bounds per-site attribution noise over four traced steps at ~0.6 ms, so `out_fwd` and `out_dgrad` are at or below it and only the three larger figures are resolved. The e2e number does not depend on this breakdown — it is 18 paired steps against 4 traced ones — but a future re-tune should be judged in situ, not on the sweep's per-call ratios.<br><br>**Numerics: two pins are bit-identical, three are not, and the distinction is deliberate.** `uvqk_wgrad` and `out_wgrad` reproduce the default bit for bit at every realistic token count. The other three differ by **one to two bf16 ULPs** (worst relative difference **4.2e-3** on `out_fwd`, against bf16's own ~0.4% quantisation step), which is ordinary reassociation for this dtype and cannot be avoided: tuning the same shapes at 1e-30 tolerance returns `Default`, i.e. no alternative solution reproduces the heuristic exactly. `GR_PIN_BF16_BLAS_SOLUTIONS="uvqk_wgrad,out_wgrad"` selects the strictly bit-identical subset, worth **1.86%**, for a run that must not change numerics at all.<br><br>**Convergence says the ULP difference does not become a trajectory difference.** Three seeds under the canonical protocol (`START_TS=0 NUM_TRAIN_TS=299`, GBS 8192, `AUC_THRESHOLD=0.75`, eval grid `EVAL_EVERY_DATA_PCT=0.001`), against a paired trunk arm with the pins forced off — jobs 31026/31027/31028 (trunk) and 31036/31037/31044 (pinned). All six logged `run_stop status=success`, and the pinned arms crossed at the **identical** sample counts and the identical AUC, seed for seed: **68.81 / 75.69 / 61.93 M** samples at AUC **0.7520 / 0.7515 / 0.7505**. Every seed inside the frozen 20-seed RCP range **61.9–80.3 M**, mean **68.81 M** against the sweep's 69.3 M. Same claim as the 2026-08-25 row: not that three seeds landed inside a wide range, but that turning the pins on did not move which eval bucket they landed in.<br><br>**Do not re-tune these without a numerical check — the first version of this table trained to NaN.** The original sweep ran without `PYTORCH_TUNABLEOP_NUMERICAL_CHECK`, so nothing stopped a solution winning on speed alone, and the one it picked for `uvqk_dgrad` (`Gemm_Rocblas_439683`) simply **computes the wrong answer** for that shape. It was worth 12% e2e and drove eval NE to **NaN within one eval**; per-pin bisection put it on that pin alone, with the other five leaving the training curve unchanged. Both rocBLAS picks are gone and every shipped entry is hipBLASLt.<br><br>**But do not trust the sweep's *timings* either, which is the less obvious half.** With the numerical check on, the sweep picked `Gemm_Hipblaslt_437878` for `uvqk_fwd` and reported 4.68 ms. Re-measured against a reference at six token counts it runs **9.82 / 6.28 / 9.36 / 8.31 ms** where the *default* runs 8.36 / 8.41 / 7.93 / 7.02 — i.e. mostly slower than doing nothing. `437615` beats the default at every size (6.12 / 6.24 / 5.83 / 5.24) and produces output identical to `437878`'s to the last digit, so it is the same math on a better schedule, and it is what ships. **Check a tuning winner by re-measuring it before shipping it.**<br><br>**A pin is only honoured if it is installed before the first GEMM at that token count**, because TunableOp fixes an operator's solution the first time it resolves a given key. That is why the hook sits immediately ahead of the projection in `_HSTUPreprocessAndAttentionFunction.forward` rather than anywhere more convenient — an earlier version hooked `hstu_compute_uqvk`, which is not the path `HSTU_HAMMER_KERNEL=TRITON` production takes, and the A/B came back a clean null. Registering at the forward covers the backward GEMMs too, since all the pins go in together.<br><br>**It fails safe rather than loud, which is the opposite of the kernel rows above, and deliberately so.** A solution index means nothing except on the library build it was measured on, so the pins refuse to install unless the TunableOp validators match `EXPECTED_ENV` exactly (torch, HIP, hipBLASLt, rocBLAS, `gfx950`). On any mismatch this is a no-op and the heuristic runs — which is merely today's behaviour, so a wrong-stack fallback costs performance and nothing else, whereas *applying* a stale index could select an arbitrary kernel. Same reasoning for the merge itself: a failed `read_file` is logged and swallowed, never allowed to take a training job down over a performance hint.<br><br>**Measurement trap worth knowing.** An in-process before/after — toggling `tunable.enable()` around a call — silently measures the *same* kernel twice, because TunableOp locks an operator's solution once it resolves the key. An early round of "bit-identical" results rested on exactly that and had to be thrown out. Every number in this row captures its reference in a **separate process**, before the pin is installed.<br><br>**This row's gain is measured with FP8 off, and it is smaller with FP8 on — the two rows overlap and must not be added.** Four arms, one node, three interleaved rounds of 200 steps, 57 matched steps: the pins are worth **−3.66%** against a BF16 control (362.94 → 349.66 `ms/step`, t = **−10.33**, faster at 51/57, reproducing this row's −4.05% on a different node) and **−2.86%** against an FP8 control (343.26 → 333.45, t = **−7.89**, 53/57). So about a quarter of the pin gain, **−0.80 percentage points**, is gain the FP8 row also claims. The combined step reconciles from both directions — 362.94 → 349.66 → 333.45 taking pins first, 362.94 → 343.26 → 333.45 taking FP8 first — which is the check that the four arms were what they claimed.<br><br>**The `BF16` in the knob's name is a limit rather than a label** — every key in the table is a BFloat16 TunableOp signature, and a name is read at every call site while a caveat in this row is read once. The mechanism is that FP8 replaces the GEMMs two of these pins name: with `FUSE_QKV` and `FUSE_OUTLN` the uvqk and output projections become `torch._scaled_mm` calls, and every key in the pin table is a **BFloat16** TunableOp signature that a scaled-gemm key cannot match, so `uvqk_fwd` and `out_fwd` stop applying while the three backward pins keep their value under `DLRM_HSTU_FP8_BWD=0`. The −0.80 points is measured; attributing it to those two call sites is read off the key format rather than measured per pin. **The practical consequence is that the pins stay on in both arms** — −2.86% under FP8 is not a marginal call — and that a smaller pin gain in an FP8 run is the expected reading, not a pin that failed to install.<br><br>**There is an ordering hazard where these two changes meet, and it is silent.** TunableOp fixes an operator's solution the first time it resolves a key, so a pin only counts if `install_for_tokens` runs *before* the first GEMM at that token count — which, since the key includes the jagged token count, means every step. FP8 moves the projection earlier in `_HSTUPreprocessAndAttentionFunction.forward`, into the `use_fp8` branch, so the hook has to sit ahead of that branch rather than where the BF16 path happened to put it. Getting it wrong produces no error and no log line: the pins install, report active, and miss. |

The FlyDSL backward running unchanged in both arms is the built-in control: −0.15 and +0.06 ms across pairs. Strictly less GPU work, no added launches, no added syncs, identical numerics — the step can fail to *improve* if the saving hides under overlap, but it has no route to getting worse. So the one unfavourable pair (−0.47%) is noise, and the **magnitude** is what is unresolved, not the sign.<br><br>**Summed GPU work *rises* in the FlyDSL arm, and that is not a counter-example.** Self-CUDA totals go ~1.99 → ~2.06 s per rank per profiled window (≈ +14 ms/rank/step). Ranks that finish compute earlier arrive at the collective earlier and spend longer inside it, which inflates summed work while leaving the step alone — which is why the **union**, not the sum, is the critical-path measure. The trace names it without ambiguity: across **all 40 rank-steps** of the pre-fix window the single largest GPU bubble is owned by `triton_hstu_attention_delta_bwd`, **2,764.7 ms** in total, and inside that op 364 of its 589.3 ms are GPU idle. Dropping the annotation makes it a runtime scalar — matching `_hstu_attn_fwd` and `_hstu_attn_bwd`, which never specialized on it — so Triton specializes only on divisibility-by-16 and the variant count is bounded at ~4 instead of one per distinct length. The op drops to 488 ms with **2.6 ms** idle. The accompanying test asserts the parameter is not re-annotated, since the regression is silent: it costs nothing in steady state and never fails a correctness gate.<br><br>**2. The FlyDSL backward synced the host once per layer** (`25a0ee9`). Two `.item()` calls per layer — `num_targets.max()` in the support check and `lengths.max()` for the block count — each a device-to-host sync draining the launch queue at its deepest point. Both recompute something the host already knows, which an audit over **3,200 calls across 8 ranks** confirmed exactly: `N` (the caller's `max_seq_len`) equalled `lengths.max()` on every single call, and `num_targets.max()` never exceeded 1. Neither fix above touches step 56, where the batch crosses a window boundary and all 8 ranks re-scan the ~350 GB event cache off shared NFS together. Staging that cache onto the node's own NVMe collapses it, reproducibly across two independent NFS→local pairs: **step-56 idle 39.0 → 6.7** ms mean per rank in the first, **26.7 → 12.7** in the second, and on the worst rank of the second **74.6 → 7.7** ms with the step itself 400.4 → 344.8 ms. That is the one thing that fixes step 56 — it is not reachable from the host-side work above.<br><br>**What it does not do is raise the rate.** Its effect on the 5-step window as a whole is inconsistent (−35% in the first pair, flat in the second, where a smaller residual boundary cost surfaced at steps 52–53 instead), and steady-state `global_sps` is unchanged either way — 20,660 vs 20,549 over steps 70–200, and three convergence seeds finished within a minute of their NFS baselines. Early windows are tiny so boundaries are frequent, but across a long run they are a small share of the steps. Weigh that against 115 s and 372 GB per cold node. This row judged that trade worth making only when profiling or when boundary latency itself matters, and **that judgment was reversed on 2026-08-25** (`9f60caf`): the cost is one-off per node and bounded, the boundary win is reproducible, and the alternative leaves every run's step-56 behaviour hostage to whatever else is hitting the filer. Nothing measured here changed — only what the default should be when the rate is a tie. |

The 90.66 ms layer saving exceeds the 80.2 ms the step actually gains: removing compute uncovers collectives that were hiding behind it, the same effect the `c088865` row documents.<br><br>**Utilization is the wrong lens for this one, and the FLOPs counter needed fixing before it could even be read.** The counter billed `n_layers ×` a full-length layer; its jagged correction is for ragged sequence *lengths*, not for a last layer that computes one *row*. What that layer actually stops executing is ~all of its attention term (one query row instead of `s²/2`), ~all of its output projection, and half its `uvqk` (U and Q collapse to one row; V and K stay full length). The discount does not have to be guessed: `fill` on the same perf line pins `K_lin·mean(s) + K_attn·mean(s²)`, and `mean(s²) ≥ mean(s)²` with `s ≤ S` boxes in the rest, bracketing it to **0.708–0.721**. So the 14.71% logged here is really **10.4–10.6%**, and HFU *fell* 12.26 → ~10.5 while throughput rose 20%.<br><br>**`a838a65` fixes the counter** (`modules/dlrm_hstu.py`, `dlrm_v4/utils.py`): `_hstu_layer_flops_targets_only()` prices the shrunken layer, and `_compute_jagged_flops_per_sample()` charges it whenever the transducer actually took that path — read per batch from the transducer, not from the env flag, so it stays right when the gate declines at eval or on a batch that is not one target per sequence. `fill` keeps its original meaning (ragged sequences against the padded budget, unchanged by the knob) and a new **`exec`** reports the share of the dense budget actually executed, so `exec / fill` isolates the work removal on its own. MFU deliberately keeps the full-length yardstick: it answers "how close to peak is the workload this model *defines*", which this knob is not meant to change the answer to — which is also why MFU *rises* to 35.09% here and must not be read as better utilization, or compared against rows that ran without the knob.<br><br>**The delta backward is already at the hardware floor**, which closes the "could FlyDSL serve it faster" question: it moves **11.17 GB** (K/V read + dK/dV write) in **2.32 ms = 4.81 TB/s**, against a measured **5.07 TB/s** device copy ceiling — faster than the 2.42 ms modelled pure-copy floor. A perfect kernel would recover **0.10 ms, 0.022% of a step**. It is memory-bound by its rank-1 GEMM structure, so there is nothing for a hand-scheduled kernel to win.<br><br>**Numerics: exact in math, reordered in arithmetic.** Forward matches the full path at **79.1 dB**. Gradients are deliberately *not* bit-identical — the surviving row is the same function of the same inputs, but its reductions run in a different order — and they land **1.5–3.5 dB below the same-arm nondeterminism floor** (`d_x` 52.4 vs 55.4, `_uvqk_weight` 52.1 vs 55.5, `_uvqk_beta` 57.9 vs 61.4). That floor is measured by re-running the *same* arm twice and is non-zero because FlyDSL accumulates dq through bf16 global atomics that reorder every launch. So the change is slightly larger than the nondeterminism production already ships with, not indistinguishable from it — for calibration, the accepted bf16 dq accumulator is a *bigger* excursion (43.6 dB against a 50.2 dB floor).<br><br>**Convergence: all three seeds cleared the bar.** `SEED=1/2/3` to AUC 0.75, the same seeds the `c088865` row used, all three logging `run_stop status=success`: **61.93 / 68.81 / 75.69 M samples** (AUC 0.7505 / 0.7520 / 0.7515), mean **68.81 M**, against the frozen 20-seed Triton RCP sweep's **61.9–80.3 M** (mean 69.3 M) on the identical 2.29 M-sample eval grid. Every seed inside the range, and the mean lands on the same 68.8 M the FlyDSL row reported, 0.20 se from the sweep mean. Seed for seed those are the *same eval buckets* FlyDSL's three landed in (61.9 / 68.8 / 75.7 M) — with a 2.29 M grid that is agreement to one eval, not to the sample, but landing in the same bucket on all three is what you would expect from a change whose gradients sit at the run-to-run noise floor.<br><br>Because the sample counts match exactly, these six runs also re-measure the speedup on their own, hour-long and on six nodes none of which was the A/B host: **1.217×** at matched steps (1.192 / 1.197 / 1.261 per seed) against the campaign's **1.207×**. Wall clock to threshold is lower at 1.183× because eval is unchanged between arms — the gate requires `self.training`, so evals run the full last layer — and 3–9 of them per run dilute the gain.<br><br>**It fails loud rather than silently declining.** The knob defaults off in code and on in `yambda_5b.gin`, and the gate has ten conditions. The nine that are fixed for a whole run (inference, group norm, non-causal, not target-aware, `max_attn_len`, `contextual_seq_len`, recompute flags, interleaved targets, non-Triton kernel) now **raise**, naming the offending condition and the opt-out — nobody re-checks a default, and a silent fallback reads as "the optimization didn't help". Conditions that legitimately vary per batch (eval, or a batch that is not one target per sequence) fall back, logged once per distinct reason. Engagement is logged once, like `[hstu-attn] backward kernel:`. |

Each arm logs which path<br>it took, so a silent fallback cannot read as "no<br>improvement".</sub> | **Closes the wrapper tax — the largest item the FlyDSL row left open.** Production never builds a packed `[N, H, D]` q/k/v: the UVQK gemm emits one `[N, 2048]` buffer and the backward gets **column slices** of it — `u[0:512) v[512:1024) q[1024:1536) k[1536:2048)`, each viewed `[N, 4, 128]` with stride **`(2048, 128, 1)`**. The kernel assumed the packed `(512, 128, 1)`, so the wrapper packed q/k/v going in and copied dq/dk/dv back out. Teaching the kernel the row stride removes all of it.<br><br>**The kernel change is a single new `rs` parameter** replacing `H*D` as the token-row stride at the 11 address sites that use it, plus the two `num_records` bounds. dO is **not** part of that buffer (it is the attention output's gradient, its own contiguous tensor), so it keeps the packed stride and gets its own base — the one place the two strides coexist. `rs` is compile-time and joins the kernel cache key, so the address math stays immediate arithmetic rather than costing registers on an issue-bound loop; with `rs` unset the emitted **ISA is byte-identical** to the previous kernel, so the packed path is provably untouched.<br><br>**Reading `uvqk` in place costs +0.16% (+0.056 ms)** in the kernel, measured paired. That is the whole budget, and it is what the removed work has to beat: per call the old path did 3 pack copies in, 3 copy-backs out, and allocated `dk`/`dv`/`dq_acc` fresh (~2.9 GB each) — three calls per step. Only dq's zero-fill remains, and it is now done in place. Within a head, D stays contiguous, and 2048 and the column offsets 512/1024/1536 are all multiples of 8, so the 8-wide bf16 loads stay 16 B-aligned; that is why the wider stride is nearly free.<br><br>**The trace shows the removed work directly.** Diffing stitched step-52 traces against the previous FlyDSL row, the HSTU attention block's *backward* drops **182.19 → 153.20** `ms/step/rank` (−15.9%) and its kernel count **247 → 134**: the ~116 copy/fill kernels that row named as the wrapper tax are simply gone. Run-wide `copy / fill` falls **513 → 400** kernels and **47.63 → 31.22** ms/step/rank. Kernel *counts* are exact and host-independent; the two traces sit on different hosts, so read the millisecond deltas as corroboration of the paired A/B rather than as an independent measurement. Attention *forward* is unchanged (−4.3%, within host noise), as it must be — FlyDSL replaces only the backward.<br><br>**It also uncovered a live i32 overflow, latent in the packed path.** `_shifted_rsrc` widened its offset to i64 *after* the caller had formed it, so `seq_start * stride` wrapped in i32 first. At production's 2.77 M tokens a 2048 stride reaches **5.7e9 elements** and faulted immediately (`Write access to a read-only page`) — but the packed 512 already reaches **1.4e9 against a 2.15e9 ceiling**, so the shipped kernel was within 1.5× of silently corrupting gradients on a larger batch or longer sequences. The base shift is now formed in i64; every in-loop offset is relative to it and bounded by `seq_len * rs ≤ 8.4e6`, so nothing else needed widening.<br><br>**Numerics are unchanged, checked against the pushed kernel directly.** Dumping every gradient under both arms — the gate's inputs are fully seeded, so the two arms see bit-identical tensors — everything deterministic is **bit-identical**: dk, dv, and the forward on both call sites. Everything non-deterministic sits at its own run-to-run floor, measured by re-running the *same* arm twice: dq scores **50.3 dB** arm-to-arm against a **50.3 dB** same-arm floor, and the downstream fused gradients land within a few tenths of a dB of theirs (`d_x` 50.3 vs 50.1, `d_norm_bias` 53.1 vs 52.7, `d_uvqk_weight` 55.5 vs 54.8, `d_norm_weight` 48.6 vs 49.2). dq is accumulated through bf16 global atomics that reorder every launch, so that floor — not zero — is the only fair yardstick, and the change is indistinguishable from it. Guard bands on the `u` columns and on 64 rows past the last token are untouched, which is the check that matters here: the risk of this change is an address straying out of its column band or off the end of the tensor, and the overflow above was caught exactly this way. The production gate (`verify_integration.py`) passes with the same digits as before: dq 43.7 / dk 83.4 / dv bit-identical, fused-path d_x 45.0.<br><br>**Layouts that cannot share one row stride still take the copy path** (checked per call: all six tensors must be `[tokens, H, D]` with packed inner dims, one common `rs`, a multiple of 8, and dq must *be* the accumulator dtype). The standalone `hstu_mha` path hits this and keeps packing — where it is a no-op, since its tensors are already contiguous.<br><br>**Tooling: no search — this one was read off a trace.** `tools/recommendation/dlrm_v4/analyze_component_breakdown.py` attributes every GPU event to the module that launched it (via the `correlation` id shared with the `cuda_runtime` launch call), which is what promoted the wrapper's +15.8 ms/step/rank and +116 kernels from "part of the attention block's total" to a line item worth fixing. The change itself is hand-written. Correctness is the repo's own `verify_integration.py` gate plus a direct arm-to-arm gradient dump. |

The winner's slowest run still beats the shipped kernel's fastest, so the floor is **+2.7%**. Numerics are **unchanged, not merely within gate**: dq 43.7 / dk 83.4 / dv bit-identical against the production golden, the same digits the shipped kernel prints, stable across three repeats.<br><br>**What changed, in descending order of contribution** — each measured as its own rung of an ablation ladder against the shipped kernel, `+1.24 → +2.42 → +2.64 → +3.51%`, monotonic with every rung separating:<br>1. **tr16 Q/dO LDS bank-conflict pad** (+1.24%). The tile is `flat[d_block][m][d%16]` with a d_block stride of `BM*16` halfwords = **2048 B, an exact multiple of the 128 B/32-bank LDS period**, so all 8 d_blocks alias the same banks — and `write_tiles` spreads consecutive threads across d_blocks, so its `b128` stores collided ~16-way. `tr_pad=8` halfwords rotates each d_block onto a distinct bank group (still 16 B-aligned, so the `b128` store/load form survives): conflicts **−43%**, LDS-active **−18%**, for **+256 B** of LDS. The ds-exchange publish now issues before ~128 MFMAs instead of immediately before the barrier, so the MFMAs cover the LDS write latency: `SQ_WAIT_INST_LDS` **−20%**.<br>4. **Redundant top-of-loop `s_barrier` removed** (+~0.5%), fused path only. That barrier exists to stop `write_tiles` clobbering `q_rm`/`o_rm` before the previous iteration's readers finish — but in the fused body the previous iteration's ds-exchange barrier *already* fires after the last `q_rm`/`o_rm` read and before `write_tiles`, and `ds_lds` is a disjoint allocation. Three full-workgroup syncs per iteration become two. **Guarded `if not fused`**: the non-fused body has no such preceding barrier and keeps its own. Barrier removal is the classic source of *intermittent* corruption, so this was re-checked three times looking for SNR drift — dq/dk/dv came back bit-stable each time.<br><br>**Three measurement lessons, all of which produced wrong numbers first.**<br>**(a) The accept threshold was inside the sampling error.** The search accepted candidates at a 0.4% paired margin from 2 measurements each. That is too loose: one accepted "improvement" of **+0.50%** turned out to be a **bit-identical file** — provably zero real change — and a bit-identical re-measure elsewhere in the ladder read +2.64% vs +2.95%, pinning residual noise at **~0.3%**. So the campaign's self-reported **+4.76%** was inflated by roughly a percentage point of noise it had banked as signal; the honest figure is the +3.96% re-measured here. Anything below ~1% needs more than 2 samples on this rig.<br>**(b) A stray edit contaminated the baseline.** The first verification read **+4.80%** because its "shipped" reference was a scratch tree an agent had written fastmath helpers into (md5 `d8a78a2d` vs the real `0860b6ab`). Re-run against the true production file it was **+2.76%**. The campaign's own seed was never affected — it came from a clean copy — but the lesson is to md5 the baseline against the *repo* file, not a working copy, before believing any ratio.<br>**(c) The first e2e round ran on clock-locked GPUs, and was far too short.** Both this row and the one above were first measured with `NUM_TRAIN_BATCHES=30` at `START_TS=150 NUM_TRAIN_TS=1` — which, at `METRIC_LOG_FREQ=10`, leaves **two** usable logged steps per arm after dropping step 10 for pipeline construction. Worse, all 8 GPUs sat in **`perf_determinism`** for every one of those arms, the exact trap [Misc](#gpu-clock-lock-can-mask-all-perf-changes) warns about: they ran 565–623 `ms/step` against the 474 this row now reports, and the throughput read swung from −1.4% to +7.6% across nominally identical pairs. Both rows were re-measured at 200 steps on restored `auto` clocks, with the perf level re-asserted before *every* arm rather than once per campaign. Neither conclusion changed sign, but the magnitudes did.<br><br>**The new kernel's timing is bimodal, and the cause is the 64 KB LDS boundary.** Across processes it lands in one of two clusters, ~34.30 or ~34.72 ms, 0.23 ms apart with a clean gap; the shipped kernel is unimodal at 0.14% over the same 10 processes. Narrowed down as follows. *Within* one process it is unimodal (0.48% round-median spread, cluster gap +0.005 ms), so the mode is fixed at process start, not drifting with clocks or alternating per launch. It is **not** a codegen lottery and **not** buffer alignment: across 10 processes the assembled ISA is byte-identical (same md5, 426 VGPR + 170 AGPR) and q/dq land at identical addresses mod 4 KB. What does track it is `group_segment_fixed_size`, swept via `tr_pad`: **65536 B → unimodal** (`tr_pad=0`, 35.13 ms), **65664 → unimodal** (`tr_pad=4`, but 44.8 ms — see below), **65792 → bimodal** (`tr_pad=8`, shipped), **66048 → bimodal** (`tr_pad=16`, 34.42/34.91). Exactly 64 KB is stable; anything past it is not.<br><br>**It is also not cheaply avoidable, which is why it ships as-is.** The shipped kernel fills 64 KB *exactly* — with `BM=64`: `q_rm` 16384 + `o_rm` 16384 + the shared K/ds-exchange region 32768 = 65536 — so there is no slack to absorb a pad. And +256 B is the *minimum* pad: `tr_pad` must stay a multiple of 8 halfwords to keep the 16 B alignment the `b128` store/load form needs, and `tr_pad=4` demonstrates the cost of breaking it — the compiler splits the accesses, registers jump to 452/196, and the kernel lands at **44.8 ms, 31% worse than shipped**. Recovering the 256 B means restructuring the LDS layout (overlapping the K tile's lifetime against `q_rm`/`o_rm`), not tuning a constant. Meanwhile the trade is clearly worth taking: `tr_pad=8` beats the unpadded 65536 B build in **both** modes (34.30 and 34.72 vs 35.13), so the bimodality costs at most 0.4 ms and never costs the win. Worth knowing for two reasons — pinning the fast mode is ~0.5% still on the table, and any future A/B on this kernel must sample several *processes*, since repeating inside one process will happily reproduce whichever mode it drew.<br><br>**Where the search stopped, and why.** 319 candidates across 6 parallel islands; 5 accepted, of which 4 were real. The last ~60 attempts are almost entirely islands *closing* axes with counter evidence rather than proposing anything: arch VGPRs are saturated at **440/512 with 0 spills at 1 wave/EU**, which blocks every read-ahead/software-pipelining variant; the 72 free AGPRs that could have absorbed them are **unreachable — FlyDSL emits zero `accvgpr` ops and its regalloc is hard-wired**, which is a DSL gap worth reporting upstream rather than a kernel problem; HBM traffic is L2-absorbed and not the bound; wider LDS reads are already `b128`. MFMA utilization is **29%** with VALU outnumbering MFMA 4:1, so the body is issue- and latency-bound, not bandwidth- or occupancy-bound.<br><br>**Tooling: [`Evolve-Kernel`](https://github.com/AMD-AGI/Evolve) for the search, `KernelForge` for the measurement.** Evolve supplies the multi-island model — six LLM agents, each pinned to its own optimization axis (algorithmic / pipeline / hardware counters / ISA / issue / DSL), proposing against a shared leaderboard and lineage, with an orchestrator re-steering them as axes close. `KernelForge` supplies the bench harness, the real-length pool and the SNR gates. Paired same-GPU A/B judging on every candidate is bolted on because absolute timings are unusable across GPUs here (3.5% spread vs a 0.4% effect floor).<br><br>**FlyDSL launches do not record into a HIP graph**, which rules out any HIP-graph-timed benchmark harness: captured graphs replay in 0.137 ms and produce no output, against 1316 ms eager. The islands were driven directly instead. |
